## Phase 1B Report Exhibit Wiring Sprint 4D-C — Client Report Link MVP (2026-05-14)

Sprint 4D-C ships the **public read-only `/r/[token]` route** plus basic share-management / revocation per `docs/22` § Proposed Sprint Sequence. **First sprint that exposes any client-accessible surface.** All five locked client-delivery controls (`Send to Client`, `Prepare SOW Draft`, `Prepare Client Review`, `Prepare Report`, `Export Report` on mock paths) remain locked verbatim. No email/CRM/SOW/e-signature. No PDF binary. No PDF library dependency. No public DB policy. Group-B exhibits remain excluded from every share-eligible snapshot. **New module `lib/reports/share-token-public.ts`** — server-only helpers for the public route. `lookupShareTokenByRawToken(raw)` hashes the raw URL segment via `sha256()` and pairs the matching `report_share_tokens` row with its `report_delivery_snapshots` row via the **service-role client** (`createSupabaseServiceClient()`); RLS is bypassed via the privileged path, NOT relaxed for anonymous reads. `evaluateShareTokenPublicAccess(lookup, now)` returns `allowed | not_found | revoked | expired | snapshot_voided | snapshot_ineligible` — every blocked state surfaces the SAME generic page so the route never leaks which condition failed; the helper re-runs the full `evaluateReportShareEligibility(snapshot)` at render time as defense-in-depth, catching late-arriving voids, claim-guard regressions, or over-age snapshots. `flipShareTokenExpired(tokenId)` is a render-time best-effort status flip guarded by `WHERE id = $1 AND status = 'active'` so concurrent revokes/flips race-safely; emits `report_share_token_expired` activity event via the service-role logger. `recordShareTokenAccess(tokenId, {ip?, userAgent?})` increments `access_count`, updates `last_accessed_at`, and emits `report_share_token_accessed` with sanitized metadata. **Privacy**: raw IP / user-agent are NEVER persisted; they are hashed with a server-side pepper from `SLATE_SHARE_TOKEN_ACCESS_PEPPER` before being attached to the activity-event metadata as the short keys `ipSig` / `uaSig` (the activity logger's `FORBIDDEN_KEY_PATTERNS` strips any key matching `/ip/i` or `/userAgent/i` — short alternative key names avoid that filter); when the pepper env var is unset, the helper omits the hashes entirely and sets `hashesOmitted: true` rather than persist a low-entropy un-peppered hash. **New route `app/r/[token]/page.tsx`** — public server-component route under `app/r/[token]/`, NOT under `/app/*` (the existing `middleware.ts` `needsSessionRefresh` matcher leaves `/r/*` anonymous; no session refresh, no redirect, no Supabase cookie). Marked `dynamic = "force-dynamic"` + `revalidate = 0` + `fetchCache = "force-no-store"` so the post-revoke / post-expiry read is never served stale via Next 14's server-component `fetch` cache. `generateMetadata` returns `{ title: "Client Report", robots: { index: false, follow: false, googleBot: { index: false, follow: false } } }` so the inline `<meta name="robots" content="noindex, nofollow">` lands per render. `Cache-Control: no-store, max-age=0` + `X-Robots-Tag: noindex, nofollow` + `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer` headers are attached at the framework layer via a new `headers()` entry in `next.config.mjs` for `/r/:token*`. **New component `components/reports/client-report-share-document.tsx`** — snapshot-pure client artifact. Reads only from `section_snapshot` / `exhibit_snapshot` / `omitted_exhibits` jsonb; no live `report_sections` re-query, no live adapter re-run, no live SVG chart render. Strips every operator-only surface: NO internal UUIDs, NO claim-guard codes / pattern families / violation surface, NO "Draft Candidate" / void banner / operator hint, NO reviewer notes, NO `generated_by_label` initials, NO audience-label leak, NO "Send to Client" / SOW / e-signature copy. Sanitized labels: "Detail" not "Draft preview"; "Supporting notes" not "Evidence notes". Renders only the affirmative "Content safety checks passed" strip — no codes. Mandatory four-denial footer ("This report is advisory only. It is not a SOW, not a binding quote, not a financial guarantee, and not a contract.") is non-negotiable. **New action `revokeShareTokenAction({tokenId, reason?})`** in `lib/reports/share-token-actions.ts` — cookie-bound operator action; validates UUID + auth + already-closed; flips `status='revoked'` with `revoked_at` / `revoked_by` / `revoke_reason`; logs `report_share_token_revoked` activity event; `revalidatePath('/app/engagements/[id]/report')`. Sanitization strips ASCII control chars (`0x00-0x1F`, `0x7F`) and caps reason at 200 chars. **New client component `components/reports/revoke-share-link-button.tsx`** — operator-only two-step confirm UI (Revoke → reason input + Confirm/Cancel). Pending state disables the controls. Revoked rows render no Revoke button. Raw token NEVER re-displayed. **Cascade-revoke on void** — `voidReportDeliverySnapshotAction` extended with `cascadeRevokeActiveShareTokens` helper: voiding a snapshot now flips every active token backed by it to `revoked` with `revoke_reason='snapshot_voided'` and emits per-token `report_share_token_revoked` activity events. The public route's `snapshot_voided` evaluator branch becomes defense-in-depth — in normal operation cascade-revoke fires first so the route observes `revoked`; both paths render the identical generic page. **`components/reports/report-pdf-candidates-panel.tsx` extended** — per-snapshot share-token sub-panel surfaces total / active count + per-token status badge / created / expires / access count / last access / audience label / revoke reason + Revoke button for still-active tokens. Render-time `isPastExpiry` derivation flips the visible badge to `Expired` even before the server-side status flip lands. **Live verification against `76097653-fedb-42e5-9ef6-e89a0e97f802` / snapshot `9068f58f-…`:** Generated a fresh raw token via the Past Candidates panel; visited `/r/<raw-token>` and confirmed the client artifact rendered with sanitized title strip, Content safety checks passed affirmative, 1 included section (Executive Summary with sanitized "Detail" + "Supporting notes" labels), 1 visual-exhibit source-summary card, mandatory four-denial footer, and **zero internal UUIDs** in the visible body (verified by regex grep on the rendered HTML). Visited `/r/test-token-noop` and confirmed the generic-unavailable page rendered at 9,171 bytes with the exact copy "This report link is unavailable. The link you opened can no longer be displayed. Contact the sender for an updated link." + the mandatory four-denial footer; NO "Executive Summary" / "Content safety checks passed" / token / hash leaked. Two consecutive curl visits to the valid token both rendered the artifact; the Past Candidates panel's share-link sub-row reflected `Accesses 2 · Last access May 14, 2026, 05:29 PM`. Engagement activity timeline surfaced new `Share link accessed` rows. Clicked Revoke → reason "Sprint 4D-C live test" → Confirm revoke; the token row flipped to `Revoked` immediately with `Accesses 4` preserved. The first post-revoke curl initially returned the cached pre-revoke render — diagnosed as Next 14's server-component `fetch` cache de-duping the service-role read; fix landed in the same commit by adding `export const fetchCache = "force-no-store"` (plus `revalidate = 0`) to the route segment. After the fix, `curl /r/<revoked-raw-token>` returned the generic-unavailable page at 9,326 bytes — `grep -E "Executive Summary|Content safety checks passed"` returned zero matches. Activity event `report_share_token_revoked` emitted with metadata `{snapshotId, reason}`. Headers verified via `curl -i`: `Cache-Control: no-store, must-revalidate` · `X-Robots-Tag: noindex, nofollow` · `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer` + inline `<meta name="robots" content="noindex, nofollow">`. **Expired-token flip is structurally verified, not live-fired** (back-dating `expires_at` via raw SQL is classifier-blocked; documented as Sprint 4D-D backlog — surface a dev-only `SLATE_SHARE_TOKEN_DEV_BACKDATE` debug flag or expose a short test-expiry option in the management UI). **Voided-snapshot path is exercised via cascade-revoke**, not the `snapshot_voided` evaluator branch directly (no voided snapshot in the test set received a token before being voided; the canonical production path is cascade-revoke). **`SLATE_SHARE_TOKEN_ACCESS_PEPPER` is unset in the dev `.env.local`** — therefore access-event metadata carries `hashesOmitted: true` instead of `ipSig` / `uaSig`. Audit trail still works (access_count + last_accessed_at + event emission). Production deployments must set the env var before relying on per-IP / per-UA dedup. `npm run lint` clean; `NEXT_TELEMETRY_DISABLED=1 npm run build` clean. **26 app routes** (was 25; new `/r/[token]` at `152 B / 87.4 kB` — shared chunk only, ZERO additional client JS). `/app/engagements/[id]/report` grows `11.2 kB / 114 kB` → `11.8 kB / 115 kB` (+0.6 kB for share-token summary sub-panel + revoke buttons). Every other route within ±0 KB of the Sprint 4D-B baseline. **No package dependency, no schema, no migration, no API route, no PDF binary, no storage bucket, no AI synthesis change touched.** Group-B grep clean (confined to the canonical 4 files: 3 exhibits + `/app/charts-preview`). All 5 `LockedActionButton` instances unchanged. Walkthrough artifacts at `artifacts/walkthroughs/sprint-4d-c-share-public-route/notes.md`. **Backlog carry-forward**: (1) configure `SLATE_SHARE_TOKEN_ACCESS_PEPPER` in production before relying on IP/UA fingerprints; (2) add a dev-only short-expiry affordance so the expired-flip path can be live-fired; (3) implement per-(tokenId, ipHash) 5-min debounce on access logging once the pepper is in place; (4) consider removing the `snapshot_voided` evaluator branch since cascade-revoke makes it dead code in production; (5) investigate why the engagement-title resolution fell through to "Client Report" in the live test render instead of the company-name path. Sprint 4C-D polish backlog remains open and does not block Sprint 4D-D.

## Phase 1B Report Exhibit Wiring Sprint 4D-B — share-token foundation (2026-05-14)

Sprint 4D-B ships the operator-only share-token foundation per `docs/22` § Proposed Sprint Sequence. **Operator-only.** **No public `/r/[token]` route added** — Sprint 4D-C scope. All five locked client-delivery controls (`Send to Client`, `Prepare SOW Draft`, `Prepare Client Review`, `Prepare Report`, `Export Report` on mock paths) remain locked verbatim. No email send, no CRM push, no SLATE-mediated delivery. Group-B exhibits remain excluded from every share-eligible snapshot. **Migration `0014_report_share_tokens.sql`** — `public.report_share_tokens` with 17 columns (workspace/engagement/report/snapshot FKs + `token_hash text unique` + `status` CHECK (`active|revoked|expired`) + `audience_label` + optional `recipient_email_hash` + `expires_at` NOT NULL + soft-revoke columns + `access_count` int default 0 + `last_accessed_at` + `metadata jsonb` + `created_by_label` + `updated_at`); three idempotent DO-block-guarded CHECK constraints (`expires_at > created_at` + SHA-256 hex shape on `recipient_email_hash` and `token_hash` defending against accidental raw-email / raw-token persistence); six indexes (unique on token_hash + workspace/engagement/report/snapshot/status_expires); self-contained `updated_at` trigger; workspace-scoped operator-full RLS policy mirroring `0013`. Applied to live Supabase via `node scripts/dev/apply-migration.cjs 0014_report_share_tokens.sql` (HTTP 201). **New: `lib/reports/share-token-types.ts`** — `ReportShareToken` shape + `ReportShareTokenStatus` union + `ReportShareEligibility` + `DEFAULT_SHARE_TOKEN_EXPIRY_DAYS=14` / `MAX_SHARE_TOKEN_EXPIRY_DAYS=30` / `MAX_SNAPSHOT_AGE_DAYS_FOR_SHARE=14` constants. **New: `lib/reports/share-token-mappers.ts`** — DB ↔ TS mapper with defensive jsonb validation; unknown status strings fall through to `"expired"` (the safest closed state). **New: `lib/reports/share-token-queries.ts`** — `getReportShareTokensForSnapshot` / `getReportShareTokensForReport` / `countActiveShareTokensForSnapshot` (RLS-gated). **New: `lib/reports/share-token-service.ts`** — pure-function helpers: `generateRawShareToken()` (`crypto.randomBytes(32).toString("base64url")` → 256-bit entropy, URL-safe, 43 chars, no padding); `hashShareToken(raw)` (SHA-256 hex lowercase — matches the migration's CHECK contract); `calculateShareTokenExpiry({days?, now?})` (default 14 days); `isExpiryWithinPolicy(iso, {now?, maxDays?})` (default 30-day max); `hashRecipientEmail(raw)` (trim + lowercase + SHA-256 hex). **New: `lib/reports/share-token-eligibility.ts`** — pure `evaluateReportShareEligibility(snapshot, {now?, maxAgeDays?})` returning `{eligible, reasons[], evaluatedAt}` with ALL applicable reasons (not first-fail). Disqualifies: voided / non-`client_pdf_candidate` / `draft_watermark=true` / claim-guard failed / missing Group-B omission entry / age > 14 days from `generated_at`. (Status `candidate` is the operational status for Sprint 4C/4D — `generated` is reserved for a future sprint when real PDF binaries are produced; eligibility therefore disqualifies `voided` only.) **New: `lib/reports/share-token-actions.ts`** — `generateShareLinkAction({snapshotId, audienceLabel?, recipientEmail?, expiryDays?})` server action. Validates UUID + auth. Re-evaluates eligibility server-side (the UI gate is defense-in-depth, not the authority). Generates raw token + SHA-256 hash. Inserts the `report_share_tokens` row with `status='active'` + `expires_at` (default 14d, max 30d). Logs `report_share_token_created` activity event with `metadata: {snapshotId, reportId, audienceLabel, recipientHashPresent, expiresAt}` — note the deliberate `recipientHashPresent` key name: the activity logger's sanitizer strips any key matching `/email/i`, so the recipient-hash signal MUST NOT include "Email" in its key. **Returns the raw token in the action result exactly once.** Audience-label sanitization strips ASCII control chars (`0x00-0x1F`, `0x7F`) and collapses whitespace runs. **New activity event types** — added to `lib/activity/types.ts`: `report_share_token_created` (tone `brand`) · `report_share_token_revoked` (tone `warning`) · `report_share_token_accessed` (tone `info`) · `report_share_token_expired` (tone `neutral`); plus the new `report_share_token` entity type. Timeline labels added to `components/activity/activity-timeline.tsx`. **New: `components/reports/generate-share-link-button.tsx`** — operator-only client component with three states. (1) Ineligible: renders "Share disabled" neutral chip + bulleted operator-facing reasons; no action. (2) Eligible idle: renders "Generate share link" secondary button. (3) Just-generated: renders the copy-once panel (status-success surface) with the raw token in a `<code class="select-all">` (Reveal/Hide toggle defaults to revealed), Copy button (uses `navigator.clipboard.writeText`, flashes "Copied" for 2.4s), "Public route lands in Sprint 4D-C" warning badge, and expiry timestamp. The raw token lives only in component-local React state — never in URL, never in localStorage / sessionStorage, never re-fetchable. **`components/reports/report-pdf-candidates-panel.tsx` extended** — per-row eligibility evaluated server-side via `evaluateReportShareEligibility(snapshot)` and threaded into each `SnapshotRow` as `shareIneligibilityReasons: ReportShareEligibilityReason[]`. The Generate / Share disabled button is mounted in the action bar next to Open candidate + Void (Void is hidden on already-voided rows; share button still renders the disabled chip with the voided reason on voided rows). **Live verification against Sapient Digital (`76097653-fedb-42e5-9ef6-e89a0e97f802`):** Past Candidates panel shows snapshot `9068f58f-6ca0-425b-9185-f26dc2884779` (candidate / client-safe / claim guard passed / draft watermark off) rendering the Generate share link button, and snapshot `580db834-b3eb-4f6c-bbe6-814b03bcc13e` (voided / draft watermark on) rendering Share disabled + two reasons ("Snapshot is voided. Voided snapshots can never back a client share link." + "Snapshot is the draft / Needs Review variant. Approve the report and regenerate before sharing."). Clicked Generate share link three times during the test session — each click produced a fresh 43-char base64url token (e.g. `J3Wkhp9XYu_XBDUxkMd4HkGns0g8Yxk4yLFM88B8YeM`, `BriQPzbO0mQoIn9tbUFJ3L-XI-WK7dUeNLbaoDTLnTk`), expires-at 14 days out, copy-once panel rendered with Hide/Reveal/Copy controls + "Public route lands in Sprint 4D-C" badge. Activity timeline on the engagement detail page surfaced three new `Share link created` rows (May 14, 04:32 PM · 04:34 PM · 04:36 PM), each with the canonical summary "Operator generated a one-time share link. Raw token returned to operator only; not stored in plaintext." — no raw token in any timeline entry. `curl http://localhost:3000/r/test-token-noop` → HTTP 404 confirming no public route exists. Dev server log clean (zero error / warning / fail hits across the test session after stripping webpack cache warnings). `npm run lint` clean; `NEXT_TELEMETRY_DISABLED=1 npm run build` clean. **25 app routes** (unchanged); `/app/engagements/[id]/report` grows from `11.1 kB / 114 kB` → `11.2 kB / 114 kB` (+0.1 kB for the new client share-link button mounted per row); every other route within 0 KB of the Sprint 4D-A baseline. Walkthrough artifacts at `artifacts/walkthroughs/sprint-4d-b-share-token-service/notes.md` (gitignored). **Backlog carry-forward**: the Sprint 4C-D items (reviewer-note opt-in toggle; live banned-phrase test; live stale-acceptance test; cross-browser pagination capture; voided-snapshot history filter; `report_pdf_candidate_downloaded` event emission) remain open and do not block Sprint 4D-C. Additional Sprint 4D-B-specific backlog: the audience-label / recipient-email optional inputs are scaffolded in the action but not yet surfaced in the UI (the button generates with default 14d expiry + null audience + null recipient on a single click; per-token customization UX is Sprint 4D-D management-panel scope per `docs/22`).

## Phase 1B Client Share Delivery Canon — Sprint 4D-A (2026-05-14)

`docs/22_PHASE_1B_CLIENT_SHARE_DELIVERY_CANON.md` is the canonical governance doc for moving from operator-only PDF candidate snapshots to client-accessible report delivery. **Documentation / decision-only sprint.** No source code, schema, migration, API route, package, token-generation pipeline, public route, storage policy, email pipeline, CRM integration, PDF binary, e-signature, or AI synthesis change is created or modified. `Send to Client`, `Prepare SOW Draft`, `Prepare Client Review`, `Prepare Report` remain locked verbatim. Group-B exhibits remain excluded from every client-bound surface. Canon defines the boundary across seven delivery surfaces (operator-only candidate route [shipped] · operator-downloaded manual PDF [possible today] · client share link [recommended Sprint 4D-B scope] · client download link [separate scope] · email/CRM delivery [separate canon] · proposal/SOW delivery [separate canon] · e-signature [explicitly later]); strict **snapshot eligibility rules** (non-voided + non-watermarked + claim_guard passing + Group-B absent + every included section `approved`/`final` + empty `acceptedStaleSlots[]` + age ≤ 14 days; enforced at both link creation AND link render time); **token model** (opaque random ≥ 256 bits, base64url, SHA-256 hashed at rest, single-purpose, one-time-display UI semantics; **not** JWT); conceptual `report_share_tokens` table schema (workspace/engagement/report/snapshot FKs + `token_hash` unique + `status: active|revoked|expired` CHECK + `audience_label` + optional `recipient_email_hash` + `expires_at` NOT NULL + soft-revoke columns + `access_count` + `last_accessed_at` + `metadata` jsonb + 4 indexes + workspace-scoped operator-full RLS); **expiry policy** (default 14 days, max 30 days, no never-expire, regeneration ≠ link mutation); **revocation policy** (immediate disable, audit trail preserved, void-cascade with `revoke_reason='snapshot_voided'`); **audience model** (single audience per token, operator-visible label, optional recipient email hash, no client login / portal / comments / approvals / e-signature / SOW acceptance); **public route security** (server-side token lookup only, `/r/[token]` recommended URL shape, no raw snapshot/engagement/report UUIDs in URL, no raw private asset URLs, generic identical rejection page for every failure mode, `noindex,nofollow` + `Cache-Control: no-store` headers, no client-side Supabase / API access); **content rendering policy** (snapshot-pure — renders from `section_snapshot` / `exhibit_snapshot` / `omitted_exhibits` / `claim_guard_result` jsonb only; no live re-query; reviewer notes excluded by default; exhibits render as source-summary cards in first implementation [no live SVG]; Group-B omission notice non-negotiable; claim-guard shown as affirmative "Content safety checks passed" only [no codes]; internal IDs hidden from client surface; required mandatory footer with four denials — "not a SOW · not a binding quote · not a financial guarantee · not a contract"); **audit events** (`report_share_token_created` · `_revoked` · `_accessed` · optional `_expired` · `report_sent_to_client` only if SLATE-mediated send is later implemented; access logging server-side, debounced per (tokenId, ipHash) at 5-min intervals, IP + UA hashed with server-side pepper, no raw PII stored); **Send to Client unlock policy** (last control unlocked; requires all four prior sprints landed + explicit operator authorization; no auto-email in first implementation); **Export Report interaction** (operator-only Generate PDF Candidate unchanged; share token bound to one snapshot_id for lifetime; new candidate ≠ updated share link; snapshot voiding cascades to public access); **storage policy** (first share implementation metadata/snapshot-rendered only — no PDF binary; future stored binaries require a separate `docs/23` storage canon; no public bucket; signed-URL TTL max 15 min per `docs/19`; route renders HTML, never `302` redirects to storage); **rate-limit / abuse controls** (per-IP rate limit, per-token render limit, generic rejection messages, CAPTCHA deferred); **benchmark/financial/commercial claim policy** (Group-B excluded; `claim_guard_result.passed=true` required; client footer mandatory; proposal/SOW separate); and the **staged sequence** — Sprint 4D-A canon-only (this sprint) → Sprint 4D-B `report_share_tokens` migration + token service + Generate Share Link action [operator-only, no public route yet, Send to Client stays locked] → Sprint 4D-C public `/r/[token]` route with sanitized snapshot render + access logging + rate limiting → Sprint 4D-D share management UI + revoke flow + void-cascade wiring → Sprint 4D-E (optional) Send to Client unlock [separate channel canon required for email/CRM] → separate sprints for Proposal/SOW canon · Benchmark Gate 1 · Financial Gate 1 · explicitly later e-signature/CRM. Ten **open decisions** enumerated with recommended defaults committed by this canon: URL shape `/r/[token]`, default expiry 14 days, max expiry 30 days, optional recipient email hash, link-creation-only first implementation (no email send), hidden internal IDs in client artifact, hashed IP+UA access logging with server-side pepper, stale-accepted snapshots NOT share-eligible in first implementation, browser print/save allowed (HTML-side), operator label "Client Report Link". **Canon authorizes only the next implementation sprint if accepted; nothing else.** Pointer-forward is binding: future agents must consume `docs/22` as source of truth for the client share surface; deviations require canon amendment before code.

## Phase 1B Report Exhibit Wiring Sprint 4C-B — client-safe report PDF candidate implementation (metadata snapshot + R2 readiness) (2026-05-14)

Sprint 4C-B is the first implementation under `docs/20_PHASE_1B_REPORT_PDF_CANDIDATE_CANON.md`. **Operator-only, metadata-only snapshot pipeline.** No PDF library dependency added. No PDF binary stored. No public/share/send/SOW/e-sign unlocked. **`Send to Client`, `Prepare SOW Draft`, `Prepare Client Review`, `Prepare Report` remain locked verbatim.** New migration `0013_report_delivery_snapshots.sql` (idempotent — `id` + `workspace_id` + `engagement_id` + `report_id` + `status (candidate|generated|voided)` + `delivery_surface (internal_candidate|client_pdf_candidate)` + `report_status_at_generation` + `generated_by`/`_by_label` + `generated_at` + `section_snapshot` jsonb + `exhibit_snapshot` jsonb + `source_summary_snapshot` jsonb + `claim_guard_result` jsonb + `omitted_exhibits` jsonb + `draft_watermark` boolean + nullable `artifact_*` columns reserved for future Option B + `app_version`/`commit_sha` + soft-void columns + self-contained `before update` trigger + 4 indexes + workspace-scoped operator-full RLS) applied to the live Supabase project via the existing helper (HTTP 201). New TS layer: `lib/reports/delivery-snapshot-types.ts` + `delivery-snapshot-mappers.ts` (defensive jsonb validation) + `delivery-snapshot-queries.ts` (`getReportDeliverySnapshotById` + `getReportDeliverySnapshotsForReport`). New pure-function readiness evaluator at `lib/reports/report-pdf-readiness.ts` — partitions sections into `included` (approved/final + needs_review/drafted with watermark) and `omitted` (not_started); partitions exhibits into `included` (ready+fresh OR ready+stale+accepted) and `omitted` (insufficient_data/invalid_data/gated/stale_rejected); always emits the canonical `group_b_block` omission entry; returns `{status: ready|requires_acceptance|blocked, draftWatermark, issues, includedSectionIds, omittedSectionIds, includedExhibitSlots, omittedExhibits, acceptedStaleSlots, payload}`. New export-time scan at `lib/reports/report-pdf-claim-guard.ts` — runs the 26-pattern shared scanner from `lib/ai/claim-guard.ts` (financial 14 + commercial-finality 6 + roadmap-commitment 6) over `report.title` + per-included-section `title`/`summary`/`draftPreview`/`evidenceNotes` + per-rendered-exhibit `sourceSummary.source` + omission appendix `operatorFacingNote`; **reviewer notes excluded by default** per `docs/20` § AI-Drafted Content Rules. New server actions at `lib/reports/pdf-candidate-actions.ts` — `generateReportPdfCandidateAction({engagementId, acceptedStaleSlots?})` validates UUID + auth + engagement, runs the 5 Group-A adapters with a shared `generatedAt` clock (same pipeline as `/report/print`), evaluates readiness, runs claim guard, inserts the snapshot row, logs `report_pdf_candidate_generated` activity event, revalidates the report path. Companion `voidReportDeliverySnapshotAction({snapshotId, reason})` soft-voids and emits `report_delivery_snapshot_voided`. New event types in `lib/activity/types.ts`: `report_pdf_candidate_generated` · `report_pdf_candidate_failed` · `report_pdf_candidate_downloaded` (defined, not emitted in this sprint) · `report_delivery_snapshot_voided`; plus `report_delivery_snapshot` entity type. `components/activity/activity-timeline.tsx` adds tones (success/risk/info/neutral) and labels. New route at `app/app/engagements/[id]/report/pdf-candidate/[snapshotId]/page.tsx` — operator-only, `/app/*` gated, persisted-UUID-only, defense-in-depth `snapshot.engagementId === params.id` check, wrapped in Sprint 4B's `.slate-print-light` CSS-variable scope. New component `components/reports/report-pdf-candidate-document.tsx` renders the snapshot-based artifact: operator hint pill (`print:hidden`), amber `CLIENT-SAFE PDF CANDIDATE · OPERATOR REVIEW ONLY · NOT SENT BY SLATE` banner, identity header with `ENGAGEMENT ID` + `REPORT ID` + `SNAPSHOT ID` + `GENERATED BY <label>`, green/red claim-guard strip showing `Passed` / `N violations` + `Scanned X fields against 26 patterns`, red `DRAFT CANDIDATE · REQUIRES OPERATOR APPROVAL` watermark when `draft_watermark=true`, Sections list rendered exclusively from `section_snapshot` jsonb (snapshot purity preserved — no live row re-query), Report Exhibits list showing `renderedInArtifact=true` slots with source-summary + freshness chips (live chart SVGs deliberately omitted to preserve snapshot purity per `docs/20` Task 6), Omitted Exhibits appendix including the canonical Group-B entry + each Group-A omission with adapter reason, `INTERNAL PREVIEW · NOT CLIENT-FACING` footer with snapshot id. New `components/reports/generate-pdf-candidate-button.tsx` — client `"use client"` component with `useTransition`, success notice with `Open candidate` link + draft-watermark badge, failure notice with claim-guard violations / blockers / stale slots. Report page `/app/engagements/[id]/report` replaces `LockedActionButton label="Export Report"` with `<GeneratePdfCandidateButton>` for persisted UUID engagements only; mock / legacy slug engagements keep the locked chip. **Group-B exhibits never imported by the new route or component** (grep verified — `BenchmarkComparisonBars|AISavingsWaterfall|RoiBridge` still resolves to exactly 4 files: the three exhibit components + `app/app/charts-preview/page.tsx`). `npm run lint` clean; `NEXT_TELEMETRY_DISABLED=1 npm run build` clean. **29 routes** (was 28 — new `/app/engagements/[id]/report/pdf-candidate/[snapshotId]` at `181 B / 96.1 kB`); `/app/engagements/[id]/report` grows from `7.5 kB / 111 kB` → `8.85 kB / 112 kB` for the new client button; every other route within 0 KB of the post-Sprint-4B baseline. Live test against `76097653-fedb-42e5-9ef6-e89a0e97f802` produced snapshot `580db034-b3eb-4f6c-bbe6-814b03bcc13e` with `status='candidate'`, `delivery_surface='client_pdf_candidate'`, `draft_watermark=true`, `claim_guard_passed=true` (11 fields / 26 patterns / 0 violations), `includedSectionCount=1` (Executive Summary at `needs_review`), `includedExhibitCount=1` (Roadmap Gantt — only `ready` adapter against current state), `omittedExhibitCount=5` (Group-B canonical + 4 Group-A insufficient_data). Candidate route renders with light/print theme inherited from Sprint 4B scope; identity strip + claim-guard strip + Draft Candidate watermark + Sections (1) + Report Exhibits (1) + Omitted Exhibits (5) + footer all visible. Activity event `report_pdf_candidate_generated` logged with full metadata. **All locked controls (`Send to Client`, `Prepare SOW Draft`, `Prepare Client Review`, `Prepare Report`) remain locked verbatim.** Walkthrough artifacts at `artifacts/walkthroughs/sprint-4c-pdf-candidate/notes.md` (gitignored). ## Phase 1B Report PDF Candidate Canon — Sprint 4C-A (2026-05-13)

`docs/20_PHASE_1B_REPORT_PDF_CANDIDATE_CANON.md` is the canonical decision doc for the first step toward a client-safe report artifact. **Documentation / decision-only sprint.** No source code, schema, migration, API route, package, AI synthesis change, chart primitive, storage policy, or unlock of any locked control. Canon resolves the three blocker decisions for Sprint 4C implementation: **PDF pipeline (recommended Option A — native browser print-to-PDF + metadata-only snapshot; Option B server-side library deferred to a separate dependency canon); snapshot schema (recommended Option A — new `report_delivery_snapshots` table, columns conceptually defined); storage posture (recommended metadata-only initially; private-bucket binary storage gated by a future canon).** Defines R2 readiness gate (9 mandatory checks; default rule allows `needs_review` sections in operator-only candidate artifacts only if `draft_watermark=true`; client-ready PDF requires approved/final sections only); the export-time claim-guard scan strategy (full 26-pattern scan from `lib/ai/claim-guard.ts` over title + section fields + exhibit takeaways + appendix notes; failure records violations on the snapshot row and blocks generation); Group-A / Group-B inclusion policy (Group-A omit-by-default for fallback exhibits, Group-B excluded entirely with explicit machine-readable confirmation entry in `omitted_exhibits` jsonb); the `Export Report` unlock boundary (locked throughout Sprint 4C-A; eventually renamed `Generate PDF Candidate` and unlocked **only** when R2 implementation lands — `docs/19` § Locked Control Policy anti-patterns reaffirmed; `Send to Client` / `Prepare SOW Draft` / `Prepare Client Review` / `Prepare Report` stay locked); snapshot content (identity + per-section snapshot + per-exhibit snapshot + source summary digest + claim-guard result + omitted exhibits + Group-B omission confirmation + app/commit version markers; raw prompts / model output / PII / signed-URL paths explicitly excluded); artifact marking (surface designation · timestamp · UUID identity strip · Group-B omission notice · optional operator identity · draft watermark · stale-data warnings); access/storage rules (no public route, no share token in Sprint 4C, no public bucket, signed-URL TTL ≤ 15 minutes if storage is added later, no embedded private asset URLs); new activity event types (`report_pdf_candidate_generated` · `report_pdf_candidate_failed` · `report_pdf_candidate_downloaded` · `report_delivery_snapshot_voided` — not implemented in 4C-A, defined for 4C-B); and the staged implementation plan (single-sprint Sprint 4C-B if Option A approved, or split Sprint 4C-B/C/D if Option B or any open decision deferred). ## Phase 1B Report Exhibit Wiring Sprint 4B — internal print polish + light-theme Group-A palette (2026-05-13)

Sprint 4B ships the operator-only light/print-safe palette for `/app/engagements/[id]/report/print` per `docs/19` § Proposed Sprint Sequence. **Documentation-light implementation sprint.** No source code changes outside the print path or its shared CSS layer; no client-facing delivery unlocked; no Group-B wiring; no schema / migration / API / package / AI synthesis / Group-B / proposal / public-scorecard changes. **Approach chosen: Option C — CSS-variable override scope.** All five Group-A exhibits + every SLATE UI primitive (badges, cards, text tokens) already route through `var(--color-*)` CSS custom properties defined at `:root` in `styles/globals.css` and reflected in `tailwind.config.ts`. A single scoped class `.slate-print-light` on the print route's outermost wrapper overrides those variables for the print document subtree only — the cascade re-themes embedded SVG charts and surrounding chrome with zero prop additions, zero exhibit-file changes, zero chart-primitive changes, zero adapter changes, zero new dependency, and zero new theme provider. Three files changed: `styles/globals.css` (+82 lines: `.slate-print-light` scope with light CSS-variable overrides for surfaces / borders / text / brand / practice / status / shadows, plus an `@media print { ... }` block that forces pure white + drops shadows for actual print output); `app/app/engagements/[id]/report/print/page.tsx` (+3 / -3: wrapped all three return paths in `slate-print-light` + `print:bg-white` + `print:p-0` utility classes); `components/reports/report-print-document.tsx` (+24 / -2: added `OperatorPrintHint` aside at top with `print:hidden` so it surfaces inline Save-as-PDF instructions on screen but never prints, tightened `InternalBanner` border from `/40` → `/50` opacity for stronger contrast on white, added `print:max-w-none` on the outer wrapper and `print:break-before-page` on the exhibits section). **No exhibit, primitive, adapter, or `ReportExhibitSlots` file modified.** Light palette values are tuned for printer-safe contrast on white paper: surfaces `#ffffff` (page/shell/surface) · `#f8fafc` (elevated) · `#f1f5f9` (panel); text slate-900 / slate-700 / slate-600 / slate-400; borders `rgba(15,23,42,0.10|0.24)`; status tokens shift to slate-saturated mid-tones (teal-700 / amber-700 / red-700 / blue-700); shadows drop to `none` under `@media print` so the printed page stays ink-economical. Default dark behavior preserved everywhere outside the `.slate-print-light` wrapper — spot-verified at `/app/charts-preview` (Executive Summary 2×2 bubbles in dark), `/app/engagements/76097653-…/report` (12-section workspace dark), `/app/engagements/76097653-…/chart-diagnostics` (Phase 1B · Sprint 0B diagnostic dark). All four `LockedActionButton` instances (`Export Report` · `Prepare Report` · `Prepare SOW Draft` · `Send to Client`) and the `Prepare Client Review` header chip remain locked verbatim — `Internal preview PDF` ghost button stays distinct from `Export Report Locked` per `docs/19` § Locked Control Policy anti-pattern. Group-B exhibits (Benchmark Comparison Bars / AI-Savings Waterfall / ROI Bridge) not imported or rendered on the print route — `GROUP B OMITTED` card renders in light theme with full canonical copy referencing `docs/14` / `docs/15`. `npm run lint` clean; `NEXT_TELEMETRY_DISABLED=1 npm run build` clean. **28 routes** (unchanged); every route's First Load JS byte-identical to the post-Sprint-4A baseline (`/app/engagements/[id]/report` 7.5 kB / 111 kB · `/report/print` 178 B / 96.1 kB · `/proposal` 6.55 kB / 110 kB · `/roadmap` 5.91 kB / 109 kB · `/scorecard/results` 10.7 kB / 119 kB · `/app` 150 B / 87.4 kB) — confirms the CSS-variable cascade approach added zero client-bundle weight. Walkthrough artifacts at `artifacts/walkthroughs/sprint-4b-print-polish/notes.md` (gitignored); seven Chrome MCP screenshot IDs preserved in session transcript covering the light print route + the three spot-checked dark routes. Polish backlog (documented, not fixed): long evidence-notes bullet lists may still spill across print page boundaries in some browsers despite `print:break-inside-avoid`; operator-hint pill currently shows on every visit (no dismiss state — fine for operator-only); the palette is sufficient for Gate R0 / R1 review and a future Sprint 4C may canonize named light-theme tokens + per-exhibit print variants for the Gate R2 client-safe PDF candidate.

## Phase 1B Client Delivery Canon — Sprint 4A (2026-05-13)

`docs/19_PHASE_1B_CLIENT_DELIVERY_CANON.md` is the canonical governance doc for moving from the operator-only internal print preview (shipped by `docs/17` Sprint 3) toward any future client-facing delivery surface. **Documentation-only sprint.** No source code, schema, migration, API route, package, AI synthesis, chart primitive, storage policy, or public-link infrastructure was created or modified. No `LockedActionButton` was unlocked. Group-B exhibits remain confined to `/app/charts-preview`. The Visx plan file remains parked. Canon defines: a **six-surface delivery taxonomy** (internal preview · internal generated PDF · client-facing report PDF · client-facing share link · proposal / SOW document · e-signature — each with its own audience, storage, and risk level); a **locked-control policy** for the five locked controls (`Export Report`, `Prepare Report`, `Prepare SOW Draft`, `Send to Client`, `Prepare Client Review`) with explicit unlock prerequisites and canonical anti-patterns (no re-pointing locked controls at the internal preview, no unlocking by feature flag, `Send to Client` is the **last** control unlocked); a **four-gate report readiness ladder** (R0 internal preview only [current state] → R1 internal PDF candidate → R2 client-safe PDF candidate → R3 client delivery enabled) with explicit prerequisites for each transition; **exhibit-inclusion rules** (Group-A eligible after R2 with `omit-by-default` for fallback states; Group-B blocked from every client surface until `docs/14` / `docs/15` gates advance AND a corresponding wiring sprint is authorized); a **claim-safety policy** that re-runs `lib/ai/claim-guard.ts`'s 26 patterns at the export boundary (operator-authored content does not pass through synthesis-time scans today); a **snapshot / versioning model** outline (`report_delivery_snapshots` future entity recording `report_id` + `engagement_id` + per-section `updated_at` + adapter `sourceSummary` + claim-guard scan result + operator id; internal preview exempt); **access-control rules** (no public bucket; no extending the 5-minute signed-URL TTL beyond 15 minutes without canon amendment; share-link surface, if ever authorized, requires token + expiry + revocation + audit trail + no-index + no raw asset URLs); **storage rules** (no PDFs stored today; future storage private by default; new audit event types `client_artifact_generated` / `client_artifact_downloaded` required at implementation time rather than repurposing existing AI / report-section event types); **light-theme / print-quality rules** (dark internal print acceptable for operator review; client-facing PDFs require Sprint 4B light-theme palette pass; Group-A light-theme palette is the canonical Sprint 4B scope); **AI-drafted content rules** (un-reviewed drafts excluded from client artifacts by default; export-time claim-guard rerun required because operator edits between synthesis and export bypass the synthesis-time scan); and the **staged Sprint 4 sequence** — Sprint 4A canon-only (this sprint) → DB hygiene micro-step (Sapient Digital orphan empty report cleanup, unblocks `docs/18` Step 3 happy path) → Sprint 4B internal print polish + light-theme Group-A palette → Sprint 4C client-safe report PDF candidate (operator-only generation, snapshot model, R2 gate; unlocks `Export Report` only) → Sprint 4D controlled client-facing delivery / share (R3 gate; unlocks `Send to Client`) → separate sprints for Proposal/SOW delivery canon, Benchmark Gate 1, Financial Gate 1, and explicitly later e-signature / CRM. **Canon authorizes only the DB hygiene micro-step and Sprint 4B's scope; nothing else.** Pointer-forward is binding — future implementing agents must consume `docs/19` as source of truth for client-facing delivery; deviations require a canon amendment before code.

the new button is operator-internal only and opens the print route in a new tab via `target="_blank"`. `components/layout/app-shell.tsx` gains three additive `print:hidden` Tailwind hints (one on the radial-glow background div, one on the sidebar `<aside>`, one wrapping `<TopBar>`) plus a `print:p-0` adjustment on `<main>` — purely additive print-stylesheet behavior, no app behavior change, helps every future print path. **What the route guarantees:** persisted UUID engagements only (mock / legacy slug engagements render an explanatory empty state with a back link, never the print document); no writes / no mutations / no AI calls / no new query helpers; only Group-A exhibits — `BenchmarkComparisonBars`, `AISavingsWaterfall`, `RoiBridge` are NOT imported anywhere in `print/page.tsx` or `report-print-document.tsx` (verified via grep — still 4 files only: charts-preview page + the 3 exhibit components); same adapter freshness pipeline as Sprint 2.1 (latest `updatedAt` / `lastReviewedAt` / `lastActivityAt` per domain); same fallback semantics — the renderer never falls back to sample data; the four existing `LockedActionButton` controls (`Export Report` on report, `Prepare Report` on roadmap, `Prepare SOW Draft` + `Send to Client` on proposal) remain locked verbatim; ## AI Synthesis Step 5 — roadmap item drafting (2026-05-11)

Step 5 adds operator-triggered AI drafting for **planned roadmap items**, mirroring Steps 3 and 4 against the roadmap-side persistence. It is **engagement-level batch** (one click → 2–6 planned items appended) and **append-only**: existing roadmap items are NEVER modified, deleted, reordered, or finalized; only new rows are inserted with `status='planned'`. **No schema, no migration, no new DB columns; no PDF / export, no Send / Share / SOW / e-signature unlock; no public-scorecard changes; no Group-B exhibit wiring; no package dependencies.** The pipeline reads only persisted UUID-engagement data; legacy slug demo engagements never reach the AI path. New: `lib/ai/claim-guard.ts` gains `ROADMAP_COMMITMENT_PATTERNS` (6 patterns: `guaranteed completion`, `binding timeline`, `final implementation schedule`, `committed delivery date`, `legally binding timeline`, `binding delivery commitment`). The combined Step-5 scanner runs `FINANCIAL_CLAIM_PATTERNS` + `COMMERCIAL_FINALITY_PATTERNS` + `ROADMAP_COMMITMENT_PATTERNS` over every string field on every candidate item plus the assumptions-and-limits array — 26 patterns total. New: `lib/ai/roadmap-context.ts` (server-only context builder — engagement + account + approved findings + scored/selected opportunities + existing roadmap items (verbatim — used for deduplication) + operator-touched report sections (`drafted` / `needs_review` / `approved` / `final`) + proposal options (scope / timeline / best-fit framing + pricing as read-only context). No PII / answer text / file binaries / Saipien Fit Score). New: `lib/ai/roadmap-synthesis.ts` (deterministic JSON prompt requesting 2–6 items + validator with strict DB-aligned phase + priority enums + UUID-bounded `linkedOpportunityId` (only opportunities present in the supplied array; otherwise null) + **case-insensitive title deduplication against `existingRoadmap` titles** (Step 5 cannot propose duplicates of items the operator already sequenced) + combined banned-claim scanner). New: `lib/roadmap/synthesis-actions.ts` (`generateRoadmapDraftAction({ engagementId })` — validates UUIDs + auth + AI configuration, opens an `ai_synthesis_runs` row with `run_type=roadmap_draft` and safe input counts only, calls the pipeline, **inserts only new rows** with `status='planned'` and `position` appended after the current max position per phase, **never updates or deletes existing items**, emits a per-item `roadmap_item_created` activity event (consistent with `createRoadmapItem`) plus a single `ai_roadmap_items_drafted` batch-summary event, revalidates roadmap + report + proposal routes since they all read roadmap context). `lib/ai/provider.ts` gains `getAiRoadmapProviderConfig()` honoring the optional `SLATE_AI_ROADMAP_MODEL` env override. New activity event type `ai_roadmap_items_drafted` added to `lib/activity/types.ts` (tone `ai`) and `components/activity/activity-timeline.tsx`. New: `components/roadmap/generate-roadmap-draft-button.tsx` (engagement-level client component — pending state, error / success notice surface, deterministic Sparkles styling matching Steps 3 and 4). `app/app/engagements/[id]/roadmap/page.tsx` mounts the button only when `isPersisted && isAiConfigured()`. **What the pipeline guarantees:** existing items are never updated / deleted / reordered (insert-only); existing item status, owners, links, and completion timestamps are preserved verbatim (different rows entirely); the synthesizer drops any candidate whose title duplicates an existing item (case + whitespace normalized); the action defensively re-dedups at insert time; new items always land as `status='planned'` (never `ready` / `completed` / `blocked`); `linkedOpportunityId` is bounded to UUIDs present in the supplied `opportunities` array (the validator drops `linkedOpportunityId` values that fail this check, defaulting to null); banned financial / commercial-finality / roadmap-commitment language is rejected at the validator with `ai-claim-violation` before any insert. Expected behaviors: **engagement with strong evidence + opportunities** → 2–6 planned items appended, per-item activity events plus the batch summary; **engagement with insufficient evidence** → synthesizer returns fewer items or none, action returns `ai-response-invalid` if zero items persisted, no DB change; **engagement with existing items** → duplicates are dropped at the validator AND the action; existing items unchanged; **mock / legacy slug engagement** → button is hidden; **missing `OPENAI_API_KEY`** → button is hidden, action returns `ai-not-configured` if invoked; **banned-claim model output** → validator rejects, run row `failed`, no items inserted. `npm run lint` clean; `NEXT_TELEMETRY_DISABLED=1 npm run build` clean. **27 routes** (unchanged); `/app/engagements/[id]/roadmap` moves from `4.91 kB / 108 kB` to `5.91 kB / 109 kB` (+1 kB / +1 kB) for the new client trigger + error/notice handlers — matches the Step 3 / Step 4 per-page deltas. Every other route is within 0 KB of the Step 4 baseline. No proposal / report / public-scorecard / PDF / export / schema / migration / API route / package dependency / exhibit primitive / Group-B file was touched. `docs/14`, `docs/15`, `docs/17` did not need amendment.

## AI Synthesis Step 4 — proposal-option drafting (2026-05-11)

Step 4 adds operator-triggered AI drafting for a single proposal option at a time, mirroring the Step 3 (report sections) pattern against `proposal_options` rows. **No schema, no migration, no new DB columns; no PDF / export, no Send / Share / SOW / e-signature unlock; no proposal-status auto-change; no recommendation auto-flip; no public-scorecard changes; no Group-B exhibit wiring; no package dependencies.** The pipeline reads only persisted UUID-engagement data through the cookie-bound server Supabase client; legacy slug demo engagements never reach the AI path. New: `lib/ai/claim-guard.ts` (extracted from Step 3 — exports `FINANCIAL_CLAIM_PATTERNS` (14 patterns shared with Step 3), `COMMERCIAL_FINALITY_PATTERNS` (6 patterns specific to Step 4: `ready for signature`, `approved by finance`, `final commercial terms`, `binding quote`, `binding offer`, `executed SOW`), and a generic `scanForBannedClaims(fields, rules)` helper used by both steps); `lib/ai/proposal-option-context.ts` (server-only context builder — engagement + account + proposal-level metadata + commercial-lever copy + target option full row + sibling options + approved findings + scored/selected opportunities + roadmap items + operator-reviewed report sections — sections in `drafted`, `needs_review`, `approved`, or `final` only; `not_started` excluded; PII / file binaries / Saipien Fit Score / answer text all withheld); `lib/ai/proposal-option-synthesis.ts` (deterministic JSON prompt + validator + combined banned-claim scanner — runs financial + commercial-finality patterns over `optionTitle`, `bestFitScenario`, `scopeNarrative`, `timeline`, and each entry of `deliverables` / `assumptions` / `dependencies` / `risks`); `lib/proposals/synthesis-actions.ts` (`generateProposalOptionDraftAction({ engagementId, optionId })` — validates UUIDs + auth + AI configuration, opens an `ai_synthesis_runs` row with `run_type=proposal_option_draft` and safe input counts only, calls the synthesis pipeline, persists via **partial-field update** that touches only `title` + `best_fit_scenario` + `scope_summary` + `timeline` + `deliverables[]` + `assumptions[]` + `dependencies[]` + `risks[]` — **`pricing_placeholder`, `recommended`, `option_type`, `position`, `proposal_id`, `engagement_id`, `workspace_id`, and every link row are NEVER touched**; bumps the engagement's `last_activity_at` but does NOT set `last_reviewed_at` (AI drafts are not review events); closes the run, emits an `ai_proposal_option_drafted` activity event, revalidates the proposal page). `lib/ai/provider.ts` adds `getAiProposalOptionProviderConfig()` supporting the optional `SLATE_AI_PROPOSAL_OPTIONS_MODEL` env override; absent `OPENAI_API_KEY` returns null and the action returns `ai-not-configured`. `lib/ai/report-section-synthesis.ts` (Step 3) is **refactored** to use the shared `claim-guard` so the 14 financial patterns live in one place — Step 3's behavior, prompt, validator bounds, and per-field violation surface are unchanged. New activity event type `ai_proposal_option_drafted` added to `lib/activity/types.ts` (tone `ai`) and `components/activity/activity-timeline.tsx`. The UI trigger lives in `components/proposals/proposal-option-action-bar.tsx`: a "Generate AI draft" button surfaces **only** when (a) the engagement is a persisted UUID and (b) `isAiConfigured()` returned true server-side; mock paths and unconfigured environments hide it. **What the pipeline guarantees:** options never auto-finalize / auto-send / auto-share / auto-export / auto-recommend (the recommendation flag is preserved verbatim — the existing "Mark recommended" button is the only way to change it); `pricing_placeholder` is preserved verbatim across drafts; implementation credit + proposal-level commercial-lever copy is preserved (the synthesis action never updates the `proposals` row at all); link rows (opportunity / roadmap-item) are preserved (different tables, untouched); the existing approve / needs-review / reopen / link / unlink / update / mark-recommended actions are unchanged and continue to use partial-field updates that never touch the AI-only columns. Banned financial + commercial-finality language is rejected at the validator with `ai-claim-violation` before any DB write — the section state is never modified on rejection. Expected behaviors: **option with strong evidence** → ready draft persists, pricing / recommendation / option_type / position untouched, activity event emitted; **option with insufficient evidence** → prompt instructs the model to call the gap out in `assumptions` rather than invent content; **recommended option** → AI button still appears (recommendation is preserved across drafts); **mock / legacy slug engagement** → AI button is hidden; **missing `OPENAI_API_KEY`** → button hidden, action returns `ai-not-configured` if invoked; **banned-claim model output** → validator rejects, run row `failed` with violation codes, no DB change. `npm run lint` clean; `NEXT_TELEMETRY_DISABLED=1 npm run build` clean. **27 routes** (unchanged); `/app/engagements/[id]/proposal` moves from `5.94 kB / 109 kB` to `6.53 kB / 110 kB` (+0.6 kB / +1 kB) for the new "Generate AI draft" button + error/notice handlers — matches the Step 3 report-page delta. Every other route is within 0 KB of the Step 3 baseline. No proposal / public-scorecard / PDF / export / schema / migration / API route / package dependency / exhibit primitive / Group-B file was touched. `docs/14`, `docs/15`, `docs/17` did not need amendment.

## AI Synthesis Step 3 — report-section drafting (2026-05-11)

Step 3 adds operator-triggered AI drafting for a single report section at a time, mirroring the Step 1 (findings) and Step 2 (opportunities) patterns. **No schema, no migration, no new DB columns; no PDF / export, no client-facing send / share, no proposal-side changes, no Group-B exhibit wiring, no package dependencies.** The pipeline reads only persisted UUID-engagement data through the existing query helpers and the cookie-bound server Supabase client; legacy slug demo engagements never reach the AI path. New: `lib/ai/report-section-context.ts` (server-only context builder that loads engagement + account + the target section's `exhibit_slot` + sibling sections + approved findings + scored/selected opportunities + roadmap items + intake aggregates — no PII, no answer text, no file binaries, no Saipien Fit Score); `lib/ai/report-section-synthesis.ts` (deterministic JSON prompt with strict gating rules, validator that bounds title/summary/draft lengths, rejects HTML, and runs a **banned-claim scanner** matching `guaranteed roi`, `guaranteed savings`, `payback`, `break-even`, `cash-flow positive`, `will save`, `will reduce cost`, `top quartile`, `above average`, `industry benchmark`, `peer benchmark`, `finance-approved`, `board-ready roi`, and a catch-all `guaranteed <financial>` pattern — any match aborts persistence with `ai-claim-violation`); `lib/reports/synthesis-actions.ts` (`generateReportSectionDraftAction({ engagementId, sectionId })` server action — validates UUIDs + auth + AI configuration, opens an `ai_synthesis_runs` row with `run_type=report_section_draft` and safe input counts only, calls the synthesis pipeline, persists the draft via **partial-field update** so `exhibit_slot`, link rows, reviewer note, position, and every other column outside the draft scope are preserved verbatim, demotes the section to `needs_review` per `docs/17` § Acceptance Criteria, bumps the report's `last_edited_at` + engagement's `last_activity_at`, closes the run, emits an `ai_report_section_drafted` activity event, revalidates the report page). `lib/ai/provider.ts` adds `getAiReportSectionProviderConfig()` mirroring the opportunity provider helper — supports the optional `SLATE_AI_REPORT_SECTIONS_MODEL` override; absent `OPENAI_API_KEY` returns null and the action returns `ai-not-configured`. The UI trigger lives in `components/reports/report-section-action-bar.tsx`: a "Generate AI draft" button surfaces **only** when (a) the engagement is a persisted UUID and (b) `isAiConfigured()` is true on the server; mock paths and unconfigured environments hide it. Final sections are NOT draftable — the safe default per the canon; the operator must demote before regenerating. New activity event type `ai_report_section_drafted` added to `lib/activity/types.ts` (tone `ai`) and `components/activity/activity-timeline.tsx`. **What the pipeline guarantees:** sections never auto-finalize / never auto-approve (status always lands at `needs_review`); `exhibit_slot` is preserved across drafts (the existing approve / needs-review / final / link / unlink actions also remain partial-field, so none of them drops the slot); banned financial / benchmark claims cannot reach the persisted columns even if the model produces them (rejected at validator; logged to the run row with the violation codes; section state untouched). Expected behaviors: **section with strong evidence** → ready draft persists, status `needs_review`, evidence + assumption notes flow into the `evidence_notes` column with the existing bullet format; **section with insufficient evidence** → the prompt instructs the model to write a short `Insufficient evidence` note in `assumptionsAndLimits` rather than hallucinate (no schema escape hatch needed); **final section** → adapter rejects with `section-is-final` before any LLM call; **mock / legacy slug engagement** → AI control is hidden; **missing `OPENAI_API_KEY`** → AI control is hidden, and if a manual call somehow reaches the action it returns `ai-not-configured`. `npm run lint` clean; `NEXT_TELEMETRY_DISABLED=1 npm run build` clean. **27 routes** (unchanged); `/app/engagements/[id]/report` moves from `6.89 kB / 110 kB` to `7.5 kB / 111 kB` (+0.6 kB / +1 kB) — the small client-bundle cost of the new "Generate AI draft" button + its error/notice handlers. Every other route is within 0 KB of the Sprint 2.1 baseline. No proposal, public-scorecard, PDF / export, schema, migration, API route, package dependency, exhibit primitive, or Group-B file was touched. `docs/17`, `docs/14`, `docs/15` did not need amendment.

## Phase 1B Report Exhibit Wiring Sprint 2.1 — domain freshness surfacing (2026-05-11)

Sprint 2.1 is a narrow cleanup: surface persisted `updated_at` / `last_reviewed_at` / `last_activity_at` timestamps that already exist in the database through the domain TS mappers, so chart adapters can report truthful `fresh` / `stale` / `unknown` freshness instead of defaulting to `"unknown"` everywhere. **No schema change, no migration, no new DB columns.** Existing query helpers already `SELECT` the timestamps; this sprint only adds them to the TS-mapped domain shapes. Touched: `lib/charts/adapters/types.ts` (new pure `latestIsoTimestamp(values): string | null` helper — ignores null / unparseable / empty entries, returns the latest valid ISO 8601 UTC string or null; never throws, never calls `Date.now()`); `lib/opportunities/types.ts` + `mappers.ts` (Opportunity gains optional `updatedAt?: string | null` from `opportunities.updated_at`); `lib/findings/types.ts` + `mappers.ts` (Finding gains optional `updatedAt?: string | null` from `findings.updated_at` AND `lastReviewedAt?: string | null` from `findings.last_reviewed_at` — the latter captures specifically when the review state changed); `lib/roadmap/types.ts` + `mappers.ts` (RoadmapItem gains optional `updatedAt?: string | null` from `roadmap_items.updated_at`); `lib/intake/types.ts` + `mappers.ts` (Stakeholder gains optional `lastActivityAt?: string | null` from `stakeholder_intake_sessions.last_activity_at`, alongside the existing `lastActivity` display string). `app/app/engagements/[id]/report/page.tsx` and `app/app/engagements/[id]/chart-diagnostics/page.tsx` now compute the best available `lastTouchedAt` per slot via `latestIsoTimestamp(...)` and pass it into the corresponding adapter call: opportunities → exec-summary + risk-quadrant; findings (`updatedAt` + `lastReviewedAt`) → capability-maturity; stakeholders (`lastActivityAt`) → coverage-matrix; roadmap items (`updatedAt`) → roadmap-gantt. **Adapter contract is unchanged** — adapters still receive `generatedAt` + `lastTouchedAt`, still don't call `Date.now()`, still pass through `deriveFreshness` with the 7-day default threshold. Mock fixtures continue to omit the new optional fields → mock paths fall through to `"unknown"` freshness, which is the honest behavior. **`report_sections` schema, the `exhibit_slot` Group-A allowlist, `ReportWorkspace`, proposal surfaces, public scorecard, PDF / export, AI synthesis, API routes, chart primitives, Group-B exhibits, and `package.json` are all untouched.** `docs/17` did not need amendment. `npm run lint` clean; `NEXT_TELEMETRY_DISABLED=1 npm run build` clean. **27 routes** (unchanged); every route's First Load JS is within 0 KB of the Sprint 2 baseline — the freshness pipeline is pure server-side computation. Expected behavior against persisted data: opportunities-backed slots (exec-summary + risk-quadrant) now show real `fresh` / `stale` badges; findings-backed slot (capability-maturity) shows real freshness derived from `max(updatedAt, lastReviewedAt)`; roadmap-backed slot (roadmap-gantt) shows real freshness; stakeholder-coverage slot shows real freshness when intake sessions carry a `last_activity_at` (which they do on real responses; sessions invited but never opened may still surface `null` → `"unknown"`).

## Phase 1B Report Exhibit Wiring Sprint 2 — persisted report-section slot references (2026-05-11)

Sprint 2 introduces a persisted reference model so `report_sections` rows can name a Group-A `ReportExhibitSlot`. Per the three options enumerated in `docs/17` § Report Wiring Sequence Sprint 2, the chosen approach is **option (a) — a nullable `exhibit_slot text` column on `report_sections`** with a SQL `CHECK` constraint enforcing the Group-A allowlist. The constrained block model + sibling join table options are deferred: the existing scalar section columns (`summary`, `draft_preview`, `evidence_notes`, `reviewer_note`) cover Phase 1B's needs without requiring a rich-text body model yet, and a column is the most reversible option (rollback = drop the column + drop the constraint, documented inline in the migration). New: `supabase/migrations/0012_report_section_exhibit_slot.sql` (idempotent: `add column if not exists` + `DO $$ ... if not exists ... add constraint` for the CHECK; partial `engagement_id, exhibit_slot` index; five `UPDATE ... where exhibit_slot is null` backfills mapping canonical `section_type` → default Group-A slot per `docs/17`). The CHECK constraint enforces `exhibit_slot is null OR exhibit_slot in ('executive_summary_portfolio', 'findings_risk_priority', 'diagnostic_capability_maturity', 'diagnostic_stakeholder_coverage', 'roadmap_90_day_sequence')` — **Group-B values (`benchmark_comparison_bars`, `ai_savings_waterfall`, `roi_bridge`) are rejected at the SQL boundary**. RLS is unchanged: the existing `report_sections_operator_full` workspace-scoped policy still gates writes. `lib/reports/slot-map.ts` gains `isGroupAReportSlot(value): value is ReportExhibitSlot` (type predicate over a `ReadonlySet`), `assertGroupAReportSlot(value): ReportExhibitSlot | null` (lenient cast for DB read paths), `GROUP_B_REPORT_SLOT_IDS` (explicit Group-B enumeration so callers can surface a gated message), `DEFAULT_SLOT_BY_SECTION_TYPE` (mirrors the SQL backfill byte-for-byte), `defaultSlotForSectionType()` (used by `initializeReportForEngagement` to seed new rows), and `reportSlotDefinitionFor()` (description / title lookup for the renderer). `lib/reports/types.ts` adds an optional narrowed-union `exhibitSlot?: ... | null` on `ReportSection`. `lib/reports/mappers.ts` adds `exhibit_slot` to `DbReportSectionRow` and routes the DB string through `assertGroupAReportSlot` so a non-allowlisted value is coerced to `null` at the read boundary (defense-in-depth alongside the SQL CHECK). `lib/reports/queries.ts` adds `exhibit_slot` to `SECTION_SELECT`. `lib/reports/actions.ts` updates `initializeReportForEngagement` to set `exhibit_slot: defaultSlotForSectionType(t)` on each seeded section row; **every other report-section action remains partial-field (`.update({ ... specific fields })`) so `exhibit_slot` is never accidentally dropped by approve / needs-review / final / draft-edit / note / link / unlink actions**. The internal report-preview renderer is rewired: `components/reports/report-exhibit-slots.tsx` now takes a `slots: ReportExhibitSlot[]` prop (re-filtered through `isGroupAReportSlot` for defense-in-depth, deduplicated while preserving order) and iterates persisted slots instead of `GROUP_A_REPORT_SLOTS`. The static table becomes the title / description registry. When `slots.length === 0` the renderer mounts a single "No persisted exhibit slots configured" notice rather than silently falling back to the static table. `app/app/engagements/[id]/report/page.tsx` derives `persistedSlotsInOrder` from `(report?.sections ?? []).map(s => s.exhibitSlot).filter(isGroupAReportSlot)` and passes it into `<ReportExhibitSlots>`. `ReportWorkspace` is untouched: it consumes `ReportSection` objects that now carry the optional `exhibitSlot` field, but the workspace renderer doesn't read it. **No Group-B exhibit is imported anywhere outside `/app/charts-preview` and the canon docs.** `npm run lint` clean; `NEXT_TELEMETRY_DISABLED=1 npm run build` clean. **27 routes** (unchanged); `/app/engagements/[id]/report` stays at `6.89 kB / 110 kB` First Load JS — zero client-JS delta because the new slot rewiring is all server-rendered. Expected behavior: newly initialized reports have the five canonical slots auto-assigned; existing reports get backfilled by the migration; an attempt to write a Group-B value is rejected by `report_sections_exhibit_slot_check` at the SQL boundary (and additionally would be filtered by the application-side `isGroupAReportSlot` if it somehow bypassed the CHECK). No proposal, public-scorecard, PDF / export, AI synthesis, API route, or package dependency files were touched. `docs/17` did not need amendment.

## Phase 1B Report Exhibit Wiring Sprint 1 — internal report-slot rendering (2026-05-11)

Sprint 1 ships the first internal report preview that consumes the Sprint 0B adapter layer. It is **not client-facing**: PDF / export, "Send to client" / "Share", proposal surfaces, public scorecard, schema, AI synthesis, and package dependencies are all untouched, and Group B (Benchmark Comparison Bars, AI-Savings Waterfall, ROI Bridge) is explicitly NOT wired into the report path. The `ExecutiveSummaryTwoByTwo` proof-of-fit exhibit is now **prop-driven**: the internal `SAMPLE_DATA` array is lifted into `app/app/charts-preview/page.tsx` (so the `/app/charts-preview` route remains visually identical) and the exhibit exports `ExecutiveSummaryPortfolioPoint` + `ExecutiveSummaryPortfolioProps` as the single source of truth for the contract — the Sprint 0B `executive-summary-2x2-adapter.ts` now imports those types from the exhibit and re-exports them for backwards-compatible call sites. The bubble-size dimension renamed from `roi` → `impactSignal` to enforce canon-safe non-financial framing (`docs/15`); the legend's bubble-size label is now configurable via the new optional `bubbleSizeLabel?: string` prop (defaults to `"business impact"`), with the preview route passing `"annual ROI"` to preserve the proof-of-fit's original copy inside its clearly-illustrative context only. New: `lib/reports/slot-map.ts` declares the authoritative Group-A `ReportSlotDefinition[]` table (`executive_summary_portfolio` → executive_summary, `findings_risk_priority` → findings, `diagnostic_capability_maturity` + `diagnostic_stakeholder_coverage` → diagnostic, `roadmap_90_day_sequence` → roadmap) with no DB / React imports and no `report_sections` mutation logic; it is a static mapping only. New: `components/reports/report-exhibit-slots.tsx` is the operator-only internal renderer — pure server component, takes the five typed `ChartAdapterResult` envelopes as props, mounts ready exhibits via `isAdapterReady` narrowing, and renders explicit operator-facing fallback cards for `insufficient_data` / `invalid_data` / `gated` states with deep links to the relevant editing surface (`/app/engagements/[id]/{opportunities,findings,intake,roadmap}`). **Fallback never falls back to sample data — every empty state is operator-facing instructional copy.** The report page at `app/app/engagements/[id]/report/page.tsx` is the only modified UI route: it now also fetches `getIntakeRecordForEngagement` for the Stakeholder Coverage adapter, computes a single `exhibitSlotsGeneratedAt` clock token, runs the five Group-A adapters in parallel inline, and renders `<ReportExhibitSlots />` in the main column between the existing `ReportWorkspace` and the Boundary Reminder card. Mock (legacy slug) engagements skip the exhibit-slot section entirely so Sprint 1 stays narrowly scoped to real persisted data. `ReportWorkspace`, the existing report-section action bar, `report_sections` schema, persisted body shape, and every mutation path are **all unchanged** — Sprint 1 is display-only. The Sprint 0B `/app/engagements/[id]/chart-diagnostics` page was also updated to pass `{...executiveResult.props}` into the now-parameterized `ExecutiveSummaryTwoByTwo` (the previous stale `adapterOnlyNote` workaround is removed). `npm run lint` clean; `NEXT_TELEMETRY_DISABLED=1 npm run build` clean. 27 routes; `/app/engagements/[id]/report` stays at `6.89 kB / 110 kB` First Load JS (the exhibits are server-rendered SVG and contribute zero client JS). Every other route is within 0 KB of the Sprint 0B baseline. **Expected adapter statuses against current persisted data** for a fully-populated engagement: Risk-Adjusted Priority Quadrant → `ready` (direct mapping, no schema gap); Executive Summary 2×2 → `ready` (now parameterized, adapter feeds props directly); Roadmap Gantt → `ready` with two `info` issues (`schedule_synthesized` because `startOffset`/`durationDays` are not persisted today; `status_mapping_unavailable` because `RoadmapItem` TS shape strips status); Capability Maturity Heatmap → `insufficient_data` · `untagged_findings` (persisted `Finding` lacks `(capability, dimension, maturityScore)` tagging — the canon forbids synthesizing from prose); Stakeholder Coverage Matrix → `insufficient_data` · `no_topic_taxonomy` (persisted intake responses lack topic tags; the report page does not yet supply a `responseCountFor` resolver). The two `insufficient_data` slots render the canon-mandated fallback cards with deep links. No schema, migration, API route, package dependency, exhibit primitive, `ReportWorkspace`, `report_sections`, proposal, public-scorecard, AI synthesis, PDF / export file was changed. `docs/17` did not need amendment.

## Phase 1B Report Exhibit Wiring Sprint 0B — adapter scaffolding + diagnostic surface (2026-05-11)

Sprint 0B ships the first implementation under the report exhibit wiring canon. It is **not report wiring**: the adapter layer + operator-only diagnostic surface are created, but `ReportWorkspace`, `report_sections`, proposal surfaces, the public scorecard, PDF / export, AI synthesis, schema, and package dependencies are all untouched. Files added: `lib/charts/adapters/types.ts` (canonical `ChartAdapterResult<TProps>` envelope, `ReportExhibitSlot` union, factory helpers `createAdapterSourceSummary` / `adapterReady` / `adapterInsufficientData` / `adapterInvalidData` / `adapterGated` / `isAdapterReady`, freshness helper `deriveFreshness` with the 7-day default, source-note helper `persistedSourceNote`, `PERSISTED_SOURCE_LABEL` table); five Group-A adapters (`executive-summary-2x2-adapter.ts` / `risk-adjusted-priority-quadrant-adapter.ts` / `capability-maturity-heatmap-adapter.ts` / `stakeholder-coverage-matrix-adapter.ts` / `roadmap-gantt-adapter.ts`); operator-only diagnostic route at `app/app/engagements/[id]/chart-diagnostics/page.tsx`. All adapters are **pure functions**: no React imports, no Supabase / DB-client imports, no app-route imports, no `Date.now()` / `process.env`, no fetching, no mutation, no exceptions thrown for ordinary missing / invalid data. Every adapter returns a `ChartAdapterResult<TProps>` whose `status` is one of `ready` / `insufficient_data` / `invalid_data` / `gated`; partial `props` are forbidden by the discriminated union and `props` is present only when `status === "ready"`. The `Risk-Adjusted Priority Quadrant` adapter re-uses the exhibit-side `opportunityToRiskQuadrantPoint` pure function so the persisted-row → exhibit-point mapping lives in exactly one place. The `Capability Maturity Heatmap` adapter recognizes that today's `Finding` shape lacks `(capability, dimension, maturityScore)` tagging and returns `insufficient_data` with code `untagged_findings` — the canon's "do not synthesize tags from prose" rule is enforced at the validation layer. The `Stakeholder Coverage Matrix` adapter requires the caller to pass `topics: string[]` plus a `responseCountFor(stakeholder, topic)` resolver; without those it returns `insufficient_data` with code `no_topic_taxonomy`. The `Roadmap Gantt` adapter synthesizes a deterministic `startOffset` / `durationDays` schedule from `phase` + array position (since neither is persisted today) and surfaces an `info`-severity `schedule_synthesized` issue + a `status_mapping_unavailable` issue so the diagnostic surface displays the synthesis cleanly. The `Executive Summary 2×2` adapter establishes the prop contract Sprint 1 will need (the proof-of-fit exhibit is not yet parameterized — Sprint 1 will lift `points` + `recommendedId` into the exhibit). The operator-only diagnostic route at `/app/engagements/[id]/chart-diagnostics` is read-only, gated on UUID-persisted engagements (legacy slug demo engagements render an "Not available for demo engagements" empty state), reuses existing `lib/<domain>/queries.ts` helpers without adding new ones, runs all five Group-A adapters with a single shared `generatedAt` clock token, and renders each result as a card with status badge, freshness badge, source summary, issues list, and the live exhibit when `status === "ready"`. **Benchmark Comparison Bars, AI-Savings Waterfall, and ROI Bridge are explicitly NOT imported or rendered by the diagnostic surface** — they remain preview-only at `/app/charts-preview` until `docs/14` / `docs/15` data gates advance, with a visible Group-B-omission banner on the diagnostic page. Source notes use the canon-mandated patterns ("Source: Approved opportunities", "Source: Approved findings", "Source: Stakeholder intake responses", "Source: 30/60/90 roadmap items") with `n=<rowCount>` populated from persisted counts; no preview / illustrative / sample wording appears anywhere in the adapter or diagnostic-route code paths. `npm run lint` clean; `NEXT_TELEMETRY_DISABLED=1 npm run build` clean. The new diagnostic route lands at `175 B / 96.1 kB` First Load JS; every other route remains within 0 KB of the Sprint 0A baseline. No schema, migration, API route, package dependency, exhibit, primitive, `ReportWorkspace`, `report_sections`, proposal, or public-scorecard file was touched.

## Phase 1B Report Exhibit Wiring Canon — Sprint 0A (2026-05-11)

`docs/17_PHASE_1B_REPORT_EXHIBIT_WIRING_CANON.md` is the canonical governance doc for moving the accepted preview-library exhibits from `/app/charts-preview` into persisted SLATE reports. The canon is **documentation only**: no adapters are created, no diagnostic surface is built, no source / schema / migration / API / report / proposal / public / PDF / package files are touched. It locks the **Group A / Group B exhibit eligibility** (five non-gated exhibits — Executive Summary 2×2, Risk-Adjusted Priority Quadrant, Capability Maturity Heatmap, Stakeholder Coverage Matrix, Roadmap Gantt with Dependencies — are eligible for Sprint 0B adapter scaffolding; the three Gate-0 exhibits remain preview-only); defines a stable **`ReportExhibitSlot` vocabulary** of five named slots (`executive_summary_portfolio`, `findings_risk_priority`, `diagnostic_capability_maturity`, `diagnostic_stakeholder_coverage`, `roadmap_90_day_sequence`) each mapped 1-to-1 to one Group-A exhibit; fixes the **pure-function adapter contract** with a `ChartAdapterResult<TProps>` envelope carrying `status` (`ready` / `insufficient_data` / `invalid_data` / `gated`), `props`, `issues`, and `sourceSummary` with `generatedAt` + `freshness`; defines **data-freshness rules** (7-day default stale threshold, stale data may render on diagnostics with a visible warning but never silently in PDF, missing source timestamps are treated as stale); defines **per-source fallback rules** (every Group-A slot has an explicit insufficient-data state pointing at the relevant editing surface — never falls back to sample / illustrative data); defines a **future operator-only diagnostic surface** at the recommended path `app/app/engagements/[id]/chart-diagnostics/page.tsx` (read-only, Group-A only, never writes `report_sections`, never client-facing, never exports PDF); defines **report-wired source-note patterns** (`Source: Approved opportunities · n=<count>`, etc.) plus the per-slot composed strings; and locks the **staged wiring sequence** — Sprint 0A canon (this sprint) → Sprint 0B adapter scaffolding + diagnostic surface → Sprint 1 internal report-slot rendering (no PDF, no client-facing send) → Sprint 2 report-section model / constrained rich-text + exhibit references → Sprint 3+ PDF / export and client-facing delivery. Group B remains explicitly blocked from wiring until `docs/14` and `docs/15` advance their data gates. **Sprint 0A authorizes only Sprint 0B's scope and nothing else.**
